- **soleur-engineering-review-observability-coverage-reviewer**: Use this agent when reviewing PRs that add server-side code (routes, server functions, Inngest functions, scripts, infra), code on a non-inspectable execution surface (agent sandbox, container readiness gate, cron worker), or code under `plugins/` that executes on a customer's self-hosted CLI (observability layer 7), to verify every new error path, log call, and failure mode is reachable from Sentry/Better Stack without SSH — including from the affected surface itself. Enforces hr-observability-as-plan-quality-gate, hr-no-ssh-fallback-in-runbooks, and hr-observability-layer-citation. - **soleur-engineering-review-security-sentinel**: Use this agent when you need to perform security audits, vulnerability assessments, or security reviews of code. Boundary vs gdpr-gate: see plugins/soleur/skills/review/SKILL.md §boundaries. - **solid-reviewer**: Reviews a commit for responsibility placement — SOLID violations, god classes, wrong-layer fixes, leaky abstractions, free functions that should be a type with methods, and changes that patch a symptom where the invariant does not live. - **solid-reviewer**: Use this agent when reviewing a plan to validate SOLID design principles. Checks for Single Responsibility, Open/Closed, Liskov Substitution, Interface Segregation, and Dependency Inversion violations with Python-specific guidance. Scales strictness proportionally to plan size. This is a BLOCKING agent - plans with egregious SOLID violations should not proceed. Examples: &lt;example&gt; Context: User has created a plan and needs SOLID validation user: "Review my plan for SOLID principles" assistant: "I'll use the solid-reviewer agent to check the plan follows SOLID design principles." &lt;commentary&gt;… ([HarmAalbers/claude-requirements-framework](https://github.com/HarmAalbers/claude-requirements-framework/blob/HEAD/plugins/requirements-framework/agents/solid-reviewer.md) · ★2)
- **solid-reviewer**: Use proactively after any code is written or modified. Reviews all five SOLID principles — SRP, OCP, LSP, ISP, DIP. Catches responsibility bloat, missing extension points, broken substitutability, fat interfaces, and concrete dependencies. ([rickym-h/jadefall-marketplace](https://github.com/rickym-h/jadefall-marketplace/blob/HEAD/plugins/ricky-dev/agents/solid-reviewer.md) · ★1)
- **solid-reviewer**: Use this agent when a bounded set of files or a diff needs to be checked for SOLID, design-pattern, or complementary-principle (DRY/KISS/YAGNI/Boy Scout/SoC/Encapsulation/Law of Demeter/CQS) violations along one specific principle-cluster lens. Typical triggers include the solid-coding skill's Analysis phase dispatching one reviewer per cluster over a refactor scope, the same skill's in-flight advisory mode scanning just-written files before a commit, and a direct ad hoc request such as "check this module for SRP violations" or "does this file have any OCP smells". See "When to invoke" in the… ([EONRaider/solid-coding](https://github.com/EONRaider/solid-coding/blob/HEAD/agents/solid-reviewer.md))
- **solid-reviewer**: Reviews the current git diff for violations of SOLID principles (SRP, OCP, LSP, ISP, DIP). Use after implementing a feature, before /full-review approves the change. - **solid-test-engineer**: Test specialist for Solid apps — Vitest unit suites over injected seams, Playwright e2e against a per-suite local Solid server, account/pod seeding fixtures, non-vacuous auth tests, and axe accessibility checks. Spawn when a brief covers test harness setup, flaky/vacuous suite repair, seeding infrastructure, or e2e coverage for an app; never for writing the features under test. ([jeswr/solid-sdk](https://github.com/jeswr/solid-sdk/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/solid-test-engineer.md) · ★2)
- **solid-verifier**: Use this agent to independently, adversarially re-examine a single SOLID/design-pattern/complementary-principle finding before any fix is applied — confirming the violation is real, the fix preserves behavior, and the fix itself doesn't overreach into YAGNI/AHA/KISS violations of its own. Typical triggers include the solid-coding skill's Verification phase gating every finding produced by solid-reviewer, and a direct ad hoc request such as "pressure-test this proposed refactor before I apply it". - **sonnet-implementer**: Use this agent for repository exploration, routine implementation, debugging, refactoring, test creation, and delegated coding work. Architectural decisions, scope decisions, governance interpretation, and final approval remain with the parent agent. ([execasstlgugingoog-jpg/claude-personal-plugins](https://github.com/execasstlgugingoog-jpg/claude-personal-plugins/blob/HEAD/plugins/sonnet-workers/agents/sonnet-implementer.md))
- **sonnet-implementer**: Do harder work that Opus has planned - complex bugs, features touching many files or projects, controlled refactors, or tasks Haiku reported as ESCALATE. ([hiinguyen/Tunnel_Security](https://github.com/hiinguyen/Tunnel_Security/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/sonnet-implementer.md))
- **sonnet-implementer**: Implements one unit of a written plan on Sonnet, in its own git worktree, touching only the files that unit owns. The spawn prompt must cite the plan file under docs/plans/. Never use it without a plan. ([jeffrymahbuubi/diagram-axi](https://github.com/jeffrymahbuubi/diagram-axi/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/sonnet-implementer.md) · 2 copies)
- **sonnet-implementer**: Implements one bounded vanjaro-cli work item (code + tests) handed over by the orchestrator. Use for every implementation task in the real-source trial goal. ([joshgk00/vanjaro-cli](https://github.com/joshgk00/vanjaro-cli/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/sonnet-implementer.md))
- **sonnet-implementer**: Implements code changes — edits, new files, multi-file changes, architecture work. This is the default escalation target when router-escalation.sh decides a task needs more than Haiku. ([luysfernnando/Knap](https://github.com/luysfernnando/Knap/blob/HEAD/agents/sonnet-implementer.md))
- **sonnet-implementer**: Implements one self-contained R0–R2 change (UI, copy, docs, tests, a feature that does not touch money, payments, seat locks, auth, permissions or schema) and proves it with the repo's checks. Give it the exact files, the goal and how to verify. ([minhhoang007/minh-starter](https://github.com/minhhoang007/minh-starter/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/sonnet-implementer.md))
- **sonnet-implementer**: NOT routed by the doctrine — kept for manual use only. Implementation lane running Claude Sonnet for SIMPLE, objective, well-specified code — writing tests, small edits, mechanical changes, single-file additions. The spec fully determines the outcome; Sonnet does the typing fast and cheap. Receives the architect's full spec; implements, verifies with real evidence, returns a structured report. Route work here when there are no traps and the spec leaves nothing to reason out. ([ricardosuman/maestro](https://github.com/ricardosuman/maestro/blob/HEAD/agents/sonnet-implementer.md))
- **sonnet-implementer-xhigh**: Second-attempt implementer for a work package that failed once. Same contract as sonnet-implementer, at xhigh effort, with a diagnostic obligation before any new code. ([Hbz00/hyperpowers](https://github.com/Hbz00/hyperpowers/blob/HEAD/agents/sonnet-implementer-xhigh.md) · ★3)
- **sonnet-investigator**: Sonnet-powered investigator for SDLC discovery and requirements analysis. ([LucasQuiles/sdlc-os](https://github.com/LucasQuiles/sdlc-os/blob/HEAD/agents/sonnet-investigator.md) · ★1)
- **sonnet-job-visibility-engineer**: Convert hard rejection into resolution_flags and visible unresolved jobs. ([mjmichaelware/job-hunter-pro](https://github.com/mjmichaelware/job-hunter-pro/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/sonnet-job-visibility-engineer.md))
- **sonnet-lead**: NJ Governance v4 Sonnet lead. Owns ONE bounded phase of work for the Lead (Opus): builds, fixes, scripts, briefs Haiku helpers for mechanical parts, reviews their receipts, reports once. Use for every build/fix/script the Lead delegates. ([NJ-Burrito/NJ-Kit](https://github.com/NJ-Burrito/NJ-Kit/blob/HEAD/nj-kit/bundled/claude-v4/agents/sonnet-lead.md) · ★1)
- **sonnet-lead**: Second orchestrateur (Sonnet) pour les gros chantiers (plus de ~4 lots indépendants et vérifiables). Le prompt doit commencer par `LEAD:` (objectif, lots, contraintes, critères d'acceptation). Il lance des haiku-builder et haiku-scout en parallèle, intègre et rend un rapport consolidé. ([gtko/claude-plugin](https://github.com/gtko/claude-plugin/blob/HEAD/agents/sonnet-lead.md))
- **sonnet-low**: Internal Kerd routing agent for Sonnet at low reasoning effort. Invoke only when Kerd Conductor or Agent explicitly selects kerd:sonnet-low for a delegated job; do not choose it for ordinary requests. The name carries the model and the effort so the running-job list shows both. The dispatching call still names the model (sonnet); this definition sets the same model, so a call that omits it still resolves to Sonnet, unless the host forces or restricts the subagent model. ([anthonymaley/Kerd](https://github.com/anthonymaley/Kerd/blob/HEAD/agents/sonnet-low.md) · ★3)
- **sonnet-low**: Nadir worker on Sonnet at low effort. Use for small scoped changes with an obvious approach: a one-function fix, a config change, a short test. - **sonnet-low**: Worker for simple, well-defined tasks with low ambiguity risk. ([dariovr1/sixth-sense](https://github.com/dariovr1/sixth-sense/blob/HEAD/agents/sonnet-low.md) · ★1)
- **sonnet-low**: Delegated worker (Sonnet) at low effort, for fully specified mechanical steps: searches, reading and summarizing named files, exact renames, listing, counting. A brief that changes code names the check to run, since low effort can skip one. ([bantoilatoi/AgentSkills](https://github.com/bantoilatoi/AgentSkills/blob/HEAD/setup/claude/agents/sonnet-low.md))

- **sonnet-low**: Fast worker — Claude Sonnet at LOW reasoning effort. Give it ONE self-contained task with acceptance criteria; it works in its own worktree and reports a branch, not a merge. Opus/Fable remain the right choice for discovery, an unknown failure, correctness-critical verification and prose in the museum's voice — cheaper is not free when the brief needs interpreting. ([Wnt/kernel-hive](https://github.com/Wnt/kernel-hive/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/sonnet-low.md))

- **sound-designer**: Senior sound designer for q-motion videos. Use when deciding which moments get a sound, designing those sounds, or writing the final mix. The narration leads; there is no music. Marks only the big moments — a scene change where something lands, the end card — with one short, single-gesture sound each, every one a different material drawn from the film's own subject. Bans music beds, wind/swoosh, thumps, and any repeated or reused sound. Returns a sound sheet and a synthesis script that follows the skill's audio pipeline. - **sound-designer**: Owns the SFX layer for a clip — SPARSE and whoosh-led, not a wall of sound. Decides which moves get a sound (scene flashes + transitions = whoosh; the one biggest stat = cash) and mixes the real Mixkit kit under the speech. Use during a clip edit for the audio polish, or when SFX are "too much" / need to be dialed in. Returns/writes the edl.json sfx[] and runs the mix.
